Use this skill whenever you need to test for or exploit the OMI vulnerability on ports 5985/5986, especially when you discover Azure Automation, Azure Log Analytics, or Azure Operations Management Suite services running on a target. This is critical for Azure penetration testing engagements where OMI services are detected. ([Cstrien/Skills_Dior](https://github.com/Cstrien/Skills_Dior/tree/HEAD/network-services-pentesting/5985-5986-pentesting-omi) · ★1)

- **omi-me**: Complete Omi.me integration for memories, action items (tasks), and conversations. Full CRUD + sync capabilities for OpenClaw. ([modbender/skill-library-mcp](https://github.com/modbender/skill-library-mcp/tree/HEAD/data/omi-me) · ★21 · 8 copies)

- **omia-database**: Query OMIA (Online Mendelian Inheritance in Animals) for inherited disorders and traits cataloged across species. The veterinary equivalent of OMIM. Use for genetic disease research and breed predisposition data. ([OpenVet-Projects/animal-meridian](https://github.com/OpenVet-Projects/animal-meridian/tree/HEAD/skills/literature/omia-database) · ★51 · 3 copies)
- **omibench-eval**: Evaluates large vision-language models on Olympiad-level multi-image reasoning tasks across biology, chemistry, mathematics, and physics. It probes the model's ability to integrate complementary visual and textual evidence across multiple images to generate stepwise rationales and select or produce correct final answers. Use when the user wants to benchmark on OMIBench, or asks about evaluating this task. Reports accuracy. ([qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool](https://github.com/qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool/tree/HEAD/skill-factory/output/omibench-eval) · ★3)
- **omic-catalog**: Use when finding, browsing, or downloading public omics datasets (GEO, SRA, CellxGene) into a local catalog. Hands accessions to sc-dataretrieval for loading. ([mianaz/sc-skills](https://github.com/mianaz/sc-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/omic-catalog) · ★3)
- **omic-signature-table-formatting**: Use when you have a muSignAl output file listing signatures with per-signature AUCs and need to extract the high-performing subset (e.g. AUC &gt; 0.95) into a ranked, exported table. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-proteomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-proteomics/tree/HEAD/collections/proteomics/v1/leaves/omic-signature-table-formatting))
- **omicmage-biological-age-multiomics**: Tier B lookup (human): what the paper reports, or simple arithmetic on values you bring; not the paper's model run on one person. Matches one person's assay names to the OMICmAge features retained in Figure 4 of Chen et al., Nature Aging 2026 (doi:10.1038/s43587-026-01073-7), and counts the All of Us inputs named for rebuilding EMRAge. Use when the user mentions OMICmAge, EMRAge, or DNAmEMRAge. Supplementary Table 3 coefficients are not in the PDF or the GitHub scripts, so the report does not print a personal age. Medicines and checkup labs do not edit that feature list. ([zwbao/longevity-skills](https://github.com/zwbao/longevity-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/omicmage-biological-age-multiomics) · ★1)

- **omics-analysis-guide**: Comprehensive decision guide for analyzing omics data (transcriptomics, proteomics) using a three-tiered approach: validated pipelines first, standard workflows second, custom analysis last. Covers quality control strategies, normalization method selection, missing value imputation, statistical test selection based on data properties, and result visualization. Consult this guide when planning a bulk RNA-seq or proteomics differential analysis to choose the right tools, tests, and preprocessing steps. Use when converting `t_metabol_01_intro` or `t_metabol_06_batch_correction` into a reusable skill, when a user has a MetaboAnalyst CSV / LC-MS peak table to clean before differential or multivariate analysis, or when choosing among PQN/TIC/median sample-normalization, log/Pareto feature-transformation, and qrilc/knn/half_min/zero imputation strategies. Use when converting `t_metabol_04_untargeted` or `t_metabol_05_lipidomics` into a reusable skill, when the input feature IDs encode `m/z`/`RT`, or when the var_names look like `PC 34:1` / `Cer d18:1/24:0` / `TAG 54:3`. Use when you have paired microbe-counts + metabolite-intensity tables on the same samples and want to find microbe-metabolite pairs that covary, or build a shared latent space.
